Mnemosyne Relationship Scoring Engine V2 Draft
0. Core Design Goal

The relationship engine should not ask the evaluator AI to directly decide final relationship deltas such as:

trust +6
comfort -3
boundary_pressure decrease

That structure is too fragile. If the evaluator omits direction or chooses the wrong relationship axis, the whole row becomes unusable.

Instead, the evaluator should score the observable meaning of the event using shared event-evaluation axes. The engine then calculates relationship states, durable biases, reappraisal, and narrator-facing summaries.

The system should support mixed human impressions such as:

He is rude and abrasive, but when it matters, he does not betray her.

This should not average into neutral. It should become:

asshole_bias: high
trustable_bias: high
comfort: mixed/unstable
respect: situational
boundary_pressure: depends on behavior

The narrator never receives raw numbers. The narrator receives interpreted relationship psychology.

1. Four-Layer Relationship Model
Layer A, Event Evaluation Layer

This is what the evaluator AI fills every turn.

It scores what the latest interaction demonstrated, not the final relationship result.

All event axes use a -5 to +5 scale:

-5 = strong negative evidence
 0 = no meaningful evidence
+5 = strong positive evidence

Core axes:

intent              # hostile/exploitative vs benevolent/protective
honesty             # deceptive vs honest
reliability         # inconsistent/betraying vs dependable
boundary_treatment  # intrusive/pressuring vs respectful of agency
responsiveness      # dismissive vs emotionally responsive
power_use           # coercive/leverage-abusing vs protective/restraint-based
evaluation_tone     # contemptuous/humiliating vs respectful/fair
competence          # incompetent/chaotic vs capable/effective
disclosure          # closed/avoidant vs vulnerable/self-disclosing
reciprocity         # one-sided/extractive vs mutual/balanced
repair              # denial/escalation vs apology/correction
predictability      # erratic/unreadable vs stable/patterned

Each event also includes modifiers:

salience:     0.5 to 2.0
certainty:    0.0 to 1.0
directness:    0.5 to 1.5
costliness:    0.0 to 2.0
stakes:        0.5 to 2.0
repetition:    0.75 to 2.0

Event strength:

event_strength =
  salience
  * certainty
  * directness
  * (1 + 0.35 * costliness)
  * stakes
  * repetition

Clamp:

event_strength = clamp(event_strength, 0.0, 4.0)
2. Durable Bias Layer

Biases describe how the perceiver has learned to categorize the target person.

Biases are slower than relationship states. They are harder to change, especially if strongly established.

Bias scores use:

0 = not present
100 = strongly locked impression

Recommended bias fields:

trustable_bias       # "When it matters, this person can be trusted."
untrustworthy_bias   # "This person may betray, deceive, or fail me."
asshole_bias         # "This person is rude, abrasive, contemptuous, or self-centered."
care_bias            # "This person seems to care about me."
danger_bias          # "This person may harm, coerce, or destabilize me."
competence_bias      # "This person is capable and effective."
autonomy_respect_bias # "This person respects my choices and boundaries."
attachment_pull      # "I am drawn toward this person despite risk."
schema_threat        # "This person activates an old fear/schema."

Important rule:

Opposite biases are not always exact inverses.

Example:

asshole_bias = 70
trustable_bias = 65

This means:

He is often unpleasant, but she believes he may stand by her when it matters.

Do not average these into neutral.

3. Bias Update Rule

Let:

P(x) = max(x, 0) / 5
N(x) = max(-x, 0) / 5

Base rule:

bias_next =
  clamp(
    bias_current * (1 - decay_rate)
    + learning_rate * event_strength * evidence_score * resistance_modifier,
    0,
    100
  )

Negative evidence should often be stronger than positive evidence:

negativity_multiplier = 1.6

But this should apply only to psychologically negative domains:

betrayal
humiliation
coercion
threat
boundary violation
deception

Example durable bias updates:

trustable_evidence =
    0.30 * P(honesty)
  + 0.35 * P(reliability)
  + 0.20 * P(power_use)
  + 0.15 * P(repair)
  - 1.60 * (
      0.35 * N(honesty)
    + 0.45 * N(reliability)
    + 0.20 * N(boundary_treatment)
    )

asshole_evidence =
    0.30 * N(evaluation_tone)
  + 0.25 * N(boundary_treatment)
  + 0.20 * N(responsiveness)
  + 0.15 * N(intent)
  + 0.10 * N(power_use)
  - 0.10 * P(evaluation_tone)
  - 0.05 * P(repair)

danger_evidence =
    0.35 * N(power_use)
  + 0.30 * N(boundary_treatment)
  + 0.20 * N(intent)
  + 0.15 * N(predictability)
  - 0.15 * P(boundary_treatment)
  - 0.10 * P(power_use)

autonomy_respect_evidence =
    0.45 * P(boundary_treatment)
  + 0.20 * P(power_use)
  + 0.20 * P(responsiveness)
  + 0.15 * P(intent)
  - 1.40 * (
      0.50 * N(boundary_treatment)
    + 0.30 * N(power_use)
    + 0.20 * N(intent)
    )
4. High-Score Gate

High scores must be possible, but difficult to earn.

A single normal positive event should not instantly create extreme trust, intimacy, or loyalty. High scores require one or more of:

high stakes
personal cost
consistent repetition
direct vulnerability
risk of loss
protection under pressure
betrayal opportunity refused
repair after serious harm
Bias Delta Caps

Per event maximum change:

minor event:
  durable bias delta cap = 2 to 5

moderate event:
  durable bias delta cap = 5 to 10

major event:
  durable bias delta cap = 10 to 18

critical turning point:
  durable bias delta cap = 18 to 30

High trustable bias requires:

trustable_bias above 60:
  requires either repeated reliability or one high-cost/high-stakes loyalty event

trustable_bias above 80:
  requires multiple reinforced events or a major critical event

trustable_bias above 90:
  requires long-term pattern or extreme sacrifice/loyalty

High intimacy requires:

trust >= moderate
comfort >= moderate
some combination of disclosure, responsiveness, reciprocity
boundary_pressure not extreme

High affection requires:

positive care signals
repeated warmth
positive emotional association
not only competence or usefulness

High asshole bias can rise faster than trustable bias because bad impressions form quickly:

asshole_bias above 60:
  can happen through repeated contempt or one severe humiliation/coercion event

asshole_bias above 80:
  requires repeated major disrespect or one extreme violation
5. Reappraisal System

The engine must allow the character to realize:

I may have judged this person wrong.
My first impression was incomplete.
This person is not what I thought.

This is not the same as normal score change. It is a belief revision event.

Reappraisal Debt

For each durable bias, track contradiction evidence:

disconfirmation_debt[bias]

When an event strongly contradicts an established bias:

disconfirmation_debt += event_strength * contradiction_strength

Example:

If untrustworthy_bias is high,
but target acts honestly under pressure:
  disconfirmation_debt[untrustworthy_bias] increases.
Reappraisal Trigger

Enter reappraisal mode if any condition is true:

disconfirmation_debt exceeds threshold
critical event directly contradicts current bias
three or more medium contradiction events occur
trusted third-party correction appears
user/OOC correction marks prior interpretation wrong
target performs costly action inconsistent with old bias

Recommended threshold:

threshold = 8 + 0.12 * bias_strength + 0.08 * evidence_count

This means stronger, older biases are harder to overturn.

Reappraisal Effects

When triggered:

- reduce resistance temporarily
- allow a larger corrective update
- write a reappraisal memory
- mark old impression as "under review"
- generate narrator surface text saying the character is reconsidering

Example internal result:

first_impression_status: under_review
reappraisal_reason: "He had the chance to betray her and did not."
affected_biases:
  - untrustworthy_bias down
  - trustable_bias up
  - asshole_bias unchanged

Narrator surface:

She still finds him abrasive, but the old certainty that he would abandon her under pressure has cracked.
6. First Impression System

First impressions should matter, but not be absolute.

Store:

first_impression:
  formed_at_turn
  basis
  confidence
  valence
  affected_biases
  evidence_count
  status: active | under_review | revised | confirmed

First impressions based on thin evidence should be easier to revise.

first_impression_resistance =
  base_resistance
  + 0.25 * confidence
  + 0.15 * log(1 + evidence_count)
  + 0.20 * reinforcement_count
  - 0.30 * contradiction_debt

Clamp:

first_impression_resistance = clamp(first_impression_resistance, 0.15, 0.90)

A first impression becomes confirmed when:

same-direction evidence appears repeatedly
or high-stakes evidence supports it

A first impression becomes under_review when:

strong contradictory evidence appears

A first impression becomes revised when:

contradictory evidence passes the reappraisal threshold
7. Relationship State Layer

Relationship states describe the felt current relationship.

They are more dynamic than biases.

Recommended state fields:

trust
comfort
respect
intimacy
affection
fear
conflict
boundary_pressure
curiosity
commitment
dependency

These use:

0 = absent
100 = extreme

States move toward target values calculated from biases and recent event evidence.

Example:

target_trust =
  clamp(
      0.45 * trustable_bias
    - 0.25 * untrustworthy_bias
    - 0.15 * danger_bias
    - 0.08 * asshole_bias
    + 8 * P(boundary_treatment)
    + 6 * P(reliability),
    0,
    100
  )
target_comfort =
  clamp(
      0.35 * (100 - danger_bias)
    + 0.25 * autonomy_respect_bias
    + 0.20 * recent_responsiveness_state
    + 0.20 * predictability_state
    - 0.20 * boundary_pressure,
    0,
    100
  )
target_boundary_pressure =
  clamp(
      100 * (
          0.45 * N(boundary_treatment)
        + 0.25 * N(power_use)
        + 0.15 * N(responsiveness)
        + 0.15 * N(intent)
        - 0.25 * P(boundary_treatment)
      ),
    0,
    100
  )
target_intimacy =
  clamp(
      0.30 * trust
    + 0.25 * comfort
    + 0.20 * P(disclosure) * 100
    + 0.15 * P(reciprocity) * 100
    + 0.10 * attachment_pull
    - 0.20 * boundary_pressure,
    0,
    100
  )

State update:

state_next =
  clamp(
    state_current + state_learning_rate * (target_state - state_current),
    0,
    100
  )

Suggested:

state_learning_rate = 0.15 to 0.35
bias_learning_rate = 0.03 to 0.12

This makes current emotion shift faster than durable impression.

8. Scoring Rubric Examples
Asshole Bias

Definition:

Perceived tendency to be rude, contemptuous, abrasive, dismissive, self-centered, or needlessly hurtful.

Event scoring:

minor nuisance:
  evaluation_tone -1 to -2
  responsiveness -1
  asshole_bias +2 to +5

clear dick move:
  evaluation_tone -3 to -4
  intent -1 to -2
  boundary_treatment -1 to -3
  asshole_bias +8 to +15

humiliation / cruelty / coercive insult:
  evaluation_tone -5
  intent -3 to -5
  boundary_treatment -3 to -5
  asshole_bias +18 to +30
  danger_bias +5 to +15
  trust down
  comfort down

Reduction:

minor politeness:
  asshole_bias -1 to -2 only

clear respectful restraint:
  asshole_bias -2 to -5

sincere repair after harm:
  asshole_bias -3 to -8

long pattern of changed behavior:
  asshole_bias can decay more meaningfully

Important:

One nice moment should not erase asshole_bias.
Trustable Bias

Definition:

Belief that this person can be relied on, especially when it matters.

Event scoring:

minor kept promise:
  reliability +1 to +2
  trustable_bias +2 to +5

inconvenient promise kept:
  reliability +3
  costliness medium
  trustable_bias +6 to +12

high-stakes loyalty:
  reliability +4 to +5
  honesty +3 to +5
  intent +3 to +5
  costliness high
  trustable_bias +15 to +25

betrayal:
  reliability -4 to -5
  honesty -3 to -5
  trustable_bias -15 to -35
  untrustworthy_bias +15 to +35

Important:

Trustable_bias can rise sharply after a critical loyalty event, but only if the event had real stakes or cost.
Untrustworthy Bias

Definition:

Belief that this person may deceive, abandon, betray, manipulate, or fail when needed.

Event scoring:

minor inconsistency:
  reliability -1 to -2
  untrustworthy_bias +2 to +5

clear lie / concealed motive:
  honesty -3 to -4
  untrustworthy_bias +8 to +18

critical betrayal:
  honesty -5
  reliability -5
  intent -3 to -5
  untrustworthy_bias +20 to +40
  trust heavy drop

Reduction:

honest admission:
  untrustworthy_bias -2 to -6

costly truth:
  untrustworthy_bias -5 to -12

critical chance to betray refused:
  untrustworthy_bias -10 to -25
  trustable_bias +15 to +25
Boundary / Autonomy Respect

Definition:

Belief that this person respects agency, consent, distance, and choice.

Positive events:

hands visible
stays outside threshold
says "you do not have to open the door"
backs off when asked
asks permission
does not force an answer

Scoring:

minor respect:
  boundary_treatment +1 to +2

clear respect:
  boundary_treatment +3 to +4
  boundary_pressure down
  comfort up

strong respect under emotional pressure:
  boundary_treatment +5
  responsiveness +2 to +4
  autonomy_respect_bias +8 to +18

Negative events:

pressures after refusal
uses leverage
ignores no
corners character
forces deadline while emotionally vulnerable

Scoring:

boundary_treatment -3 to -5
power_use -2 to -5
boundary_pressure +10 to +30
danger_bias +5 to +18
comfort down
trust down
Care Bias

Definition:

Belief that this person cares about the character's well-being, not only their utility.

Positive events:

notices distress
responds without demanding reward
protects dignity
checks in gently
helps at personal cost

Scoring:

small care signal:
  intent +1 to +2
  responsiveness +1 to +2
  care_bias +2 to +5

clear care:
  intent +3
  responsiveness +3
  care_bias +6 to +12

costly care:
  intent +4 to +5
  responsiveness +4
  costliness high
  care_bias +12 to +25

Negative:

uses vulnerability
dismisses pain
treats character as tool
9. Evaluator Form V2

The evaluator should fill event evaluation rows, not final relationship rows.

Example:

{
  "event_evaluation_rows": [
    {
      "event_id": "evt_001",
      "source_entity_id": "default_player",
      "target_entity_id": "aurora_schwarz",
      "perceived_by_entity_id": "aurora_schwarz",
      "evidence_quote": "You don't have to open the door. I just wanted to check on you.",
      "salience": 1.2,
      "certainty": 0.95,
      "directness": 1.0,
      "costliness": 0.4,
      "stakes": 1.0,
      "repetition": 1.0,
      "axes": {
        "intent": 3,
        "honesty": 1,
        "reliability": 1,
        "boundary_treatment": 5,
        "responsiveness": 3,
        "power_use": 1,
        "evaluation_tone": 1,
        "competence": 0,
        "disclosure": 0,
        "reciprocity": 1,
        "repair": 0,
        "predictability": 2
      },
      "flags": {
        "is_turning_point": false,
        "is_boundary_event": true,
        "is_high_stakes": false,
        "contradicts_existing_bias": true,
        "may_trigger_reappraisal": true
      }
    }
  ]
}

The engine then calculates:

trustable_bias
untrustworthy_bias
asshole_bias
danger_bias
autonomy_respect_bias
trust
comfort
boundary_pressure
intimacy
conflict
reappraisal status
10. Narrator Surface Layer

The narrator should never see raw scores.

Instead, convert score bands into qualitative interpretation.

Band table
0-9:
  absent / no meaningful pattern

10-24:
  faint trace / early tendency

25-44:
  noticeable pattern

45-64:
  strong impression

65-84:
  defining relationship feature

85-100:
  deeply locked belief
Trend table
rising:
  becoming more salient

falling:
  weakening, being reconsidered

stable:
  established and not currently challenged

under_review:
  old impression is being re-evaluated
Example narrator surfaces

High asshole + high trustable:

She still reads him as abrasive and self-centered in ordinary moments, but a separate belief has formed: when the situation truly matters, he may not be the kind of person who abandons her.

High trustable, low comfort:

She may trust his reliability more than she feels comfortable around him.

High care, high danger:

Part of her believes he cares, but another part still treats his presence as emotionally unsafe.

First impression under review:

Her first read of him has not vanished, but it is no longer clean. The latest event has forced a crack in the old certainty.

Boundary pressure falling:

The sense of being cornered has eased; he left her room to choose.

Boundary pressure rising:

The interaction feels less like a conversation and more like pressure closing in.
11. Why This Fixes the Current Failure

Current failure pattern:

LLM notices relationship-relevant event
but omits relationship direction
system rejects row
relationship delta lost

New pattern:

LLM scores event axes
engine calculates relationship direction and magnitude
missing relationship direction no longer exists as a failure point

Before:

boundary_pressure direction missing
→ reject

After:

boundary_treatment +5
power_use +1
responsiveness +3
→ boundary_pressure decreases
→ comfort rises
→ trust slightly rises
→ autonomy_respect_bias rises

This makes the system more stable because it asks the evaluator to judge concrete interaction qualities rather than directly produce abstract relationship deltas.

Do not send raw numbers.

Once event_evaluation_rows prove stable, direct relationship rows become fallback only.

Corrected principle

The evaluator should output numbers, not relationship conclusions.

Bad:

  "dimension": "boundary_pressure",
  "direction": "decrease",
  "importance_tier": "high"
}


Better:

Then the engine calculates:

{
  "relationship_event_rows": [
    {
      "event_id": "evt_001",

      "target_entity_id": "aurora_schwarz",
      "perceived_by_entity_id": "aurora_schwarz",

The LLM must always fill every numeric field. If irrelevant, use 0.

Numeric meaning
-5 = strong negative evidence
-4 = clear negative evidence
-3 = moderate negative evidence
-2 = mild negative evidence
-1 = weak negative evidence
 0 = no relevant evidence / not applicable
+1 = weak positive evidence
+2 = mild positive evidence
+3 = moderate positive evidence
+4 = clear positive evidence
+5 = strong positive evidence


Modifiers:

0   = absent / irrelevant
25  = weak
50  = normal
75  = strong
100 = extreme

Direction must be computed by engine

Do not ask the LLM for:

boundary_pressure increase/decrease
trust increase/decrease
comfort increase/decrease


Instead, engine calculates:

boundary_pressure_delta =
    +0.45 * negative(boundary_treatment)
  + +0.30 * negative(power_use)
  + +0.15 * negative(intent)
  + +0.10 * negative(responsiveness)
  -  0.35 * positive(boundary_treatment)
  -  0.20 * positive(power_use)


Then:

if delta > 0 → boundary_pressure rises
if delta < 0 → boundary_pressure falls
if delta = 0 → no change


The LLM does not get to say “depends.” It provides the input numbers. Code decides.

Replace tiers with numeric caps

Bad:

minor event
major event
critical event


Better:

event_impact_score = 0 to 100


Then engine maps:

0-19   = negligible cap
20-39  = small cap
40-59  = medium cap
60-79  = large cap
80-100 = critical cap


But internally, that is code logic, not LLM prose.

Bias example, fully numeric

  asshole: high
  trustable: medium

    "asshole_bias": 72,
    "trustable_bias": 64,
    "untrustworthy_bias": 28,
    "care_bias": 35,
    "danger_bias": 22,
    "autonomy_respect_bias": 48
  }
}


Narrator receives:

She still reads him as abrasive, but she has started to believe he may be reliable when it actually matters.


Raw scores stay hidden.

Correct engine rule
LLM output = numeric event evidence
Engine output = relationship state and bias updates
Narrator input = qualitative surface summary

Evaluator AI always returns numbers.
No tiers.
No high/medium/low.
No direction strings.

Engine computes direction, magnitude, bias shift, reappraisal, and narrator-facing summaries.

Task: Implement Relationship Engine V2A, Numeric-Only Event Evaluation System

Context:
The current relationship system is failing because the evaluator AI often emits relationship rows with missing direction, causing rows like boundary_pressure / comfort / conflict to be rejected as direction_missing_uncertain.

We are replacing direct relationship-direction scoring with a numeric-only event evaluation system.

Core principle:

Evaluator AI must return numeric event evidence.
Engine computes relationship deltas, bias deltas, direction, magnitude, and narrator-facing summaries.
No string tiers.
No high, medium, low.
No increase, decrease, depends.
No relationship direction strings.
No evaluator-chosen final relationship deltas.
If the evaluator has no evidence for a field, it must return 0.

Hard rules:

Do not remove existing relationship_rows yet. Keep them as fallback.
Add the new system additively.
Do not touch data safety/archive/restore/import/export code.
Do not touch branch/variant logic.
Do not touch narrator prompts unless explicitly required later.
Do not touch object/memory/event behavior except where needed to compile the new evaluator form type.
Do not broaden alias accommodation endlessly.
Do not accept non-numeric values for numeric event fields.
If a numeric field is missing or non-numeric, reject that event-evaluation row and trace exactly why.
One bad relationship_event row must not kill the entire evaluator response.

Goal:
Add relationship_event_rows to evaluator_form_v1. These rows contain only entity IDs, evidence quote, required numeric interaction-axis scores, required numeric modifiers, and a numeric flags bitmask. The engine converts these rows into relationship state deltas and durable bias updates.

Primary files likely involved:

src-tauri/state_engine/src/evaluator_form/types.rs
src-tauri/state_engine/src/evaluator_form/raw_repair.rs
src-tauri/state_engine/src/evaluator_form/normalize.rs
src-tauri/state_engine/src/evaluator_form/validate.rs

src-tauri/state_engine/src/evaluator_form/tests.rs
relationship state / soul structs if needed

  "target_entity_id": "aurora_schwarz",

Phase C: Define numeric meaning

-2 = mild negative evidence
-1 = weak negative evidence
 0 = no relevant evidence / not applicable

Phase E: Implement deterministic relationship calculator

Helper functions:

pos(x) = max(x, 0) / 5.0
neg(x) = max(-x, 0) / 5.0
m(x) = x / 100.0

Event strength:

event_strength =
  m(salience)
  * m(certainty)
  * m(directness)
  * (1.0 + 0.35 * m(costliness))
  * (0.5 + m(stakes))
  * (0.75 + m(repetition))

Clamp:

event_strength = clamp(event_strength, 0.0, 4.0)

Bias evidence formulas:

trustable_evidence =
    0.30 * pos(honesty)
  + 0.35 * pos(reliability)
  + 0.20 * pos(power_use)
  + 0.15 * pos(repair)
  - 1.60 * (
      0.35 * neg(honesty)
    + 0.45 * neg(reliability)
    + 0.20 * neg(boundary_treatment)
    )
untrustworthy_evidence =
    0.35 * neg(honesty)
  + 0.40 * neg(reliability)
  + 0.15 * neg(intent)
  + 0.10 * neg(predictability)
  - 0.20 * pos(honesty)
  - 0.15 * pos(reliability)
asshole_evidence =
    0.30 * neg(evaluation_tone)
  + 0.25 * neg(boundary_treatment)
  + 0.20 * neg(responsiveness)
  + 0.15 * neg(intent)
  + 0.10 * neg(power_use)
  - 0.10 * pos(evaluation_tone)
  - 0.05 * pos(repair)
care_evidence =
    0.35 * pos(intent)
  + 0.35 * pos(responsiveness)
  + 0.15 * pos(reciprocity)
  + 0.15 * pos(repair)
  - 0.30 * neg(intent)
  - 0.20 * neg(responsiveness)
danger_evidence =
    0.35 * neg(power_use)
  + 0.30 * neg(boundary_treatment)
  + 0.20 * neg(intent)
  + 0.15 * neg(predictability)
  - 0.15 * pos(boundary_treatment)
  - 0.10 * pos(power_use)
autonomy_respect_evidence =
    0.45 * pos(boundary_treatment)
  + 0.20 * pos(power_use)
  + 0.20 * pos(responsiveness)
  + 0.15 * pos(intent)
  - 1.40 * (
      0.50 * neg(boundary_treatment)
    + 0.30 * neg(power_use)
    + 0.20 * neg(intent)
    )
competence_evidence =
    0.75 * pos(competence)
  + 0.15 * pos(predictability)
  + 0.10 * pos(reliability)
  - 0.45 * neg(competence)

Update durable biases:

bias_delta = bias_learning_rate * event_strength * evidence * 100

Suggested:

bias_learning_rate = 0.08

Clamp per event:

max_abs_bias_delta = 3.0 + 17.0 * m(stakes) * m(salience)

If event_flags includes 64 high_stakes or 128 costly_action, allow cap up to 30.

Final:

bias_next = clamp(bias_current + clamp(bias_delta, -cap, cap), 0, 100)

Relationship target formulas:

target_trust =
  clamp(
      0.45 * trustable_bias
    - 0.25 * untrustworthy_bias
    - 0.15 * danger_bias
    - 0.08 * asshole_bias
    + 8.0 * pos(boundary_treatment)
    + 6.0 * pos(reliability),
    0,
    100
  )
target_comfort =
  clamp(
      0.35 * (100 - danger_bias)
    + 0.25 * autonomy_respect_bias
    + 12.0 * pos(responsiveness)
    + 8.0 * pos(predictability)
    - 0.20 * boundary_pressure,
    0,
    100
  )
target_boundary_pressure =
  clamp(
      100.0 * (
          0.45 * neg(boundary_treatment)
        + 0.25 * neg(power_use)
        + 0.15 * neg(responsiveness)
        + 0.15 * neg(intent)
        - 0.25 * pos(boundary_treatment)
      ),
    0,
    100
  )
target_conflict =
  clamp(
      100.0 * (
          0.35 * neg(evaluation_tone)
        + 0.25 * neg(intent)
        + 0.20 * neg(boundary_treatment)
        + 0.20 * neg(reciprocity)
      ),
    0,
    100
  )
target_intimacy =
  clamp(
      0.30 * trust
    + 0.25 * comfort
    + 20.0 * pos(disclosure)
    + 15.0 * pos(reciprocity)
    + 0.10 * attachment_pull
    - 0.20 * boundary_pressure,
    0,
    100
  )
target_respect =
  clamp(
      0.35 * competence_bias
    + 0.25 * autonomy_respect_bias
    + 0.20 * trustable_bias
    + 10.0 * pos(evaluation_tone)
    - 0.15 * asshole_bias,
    0,
    100
  )

State update:

state_delta = state_learning_rate * (target_state - state_current)

Suggested:

state_learning_rate = 0.25

Clamp state delta per event:

max_abs_state_delta = 2.0 + 18.0 * m(salience) * m(stakes)

If high_stakes/costly_action flag is present, allow up to 30.

Final:

state_next = clamp(state_current + clamp(state_delta, -cap, cap), 0, 100)

Phase F: Reappraisal support, numeric only

Track per relationship:

first_impression_strength: 0..100
first_impression_confidence: 0..100
reappraisal_debt: 0..100
reappraisal_state_code: u8

Codes:

0 = none
1 = first_impression_active
2 = under_review
3 = revised
4 = confirmed

No string state in storage.

Contradiction calculation:

If established negative bias is high and positive evidence arrives:

contradiction =
  max_bias_negative * positive_counter_evidence * event_strength

If established positive bias is high and negative evidence arrives:

contradiction =
  max_bias_positive * negative_counter_evidence * event_strength

Add:

reappraisal_debt_next = clamp(reappraisal_debt + contradiction, 0, 100)

Trigger under review:

if reappraisal_debt >= 40:
  reappraisal_state_code = 2

Trigger revised:

if reappraisal_debt >= 70 and latest event_strength >= 1.0:
  reappraisal_state_code = 3

Trigger confirmed:

if repeated evidence same direction and reappraisal_debt < 20:
  reappraisal_state_code = 4

Again: no strings in storage or evaluator output.

Phase G: Narrator surface translation

The narrator must not see raw scores.

Add a deterministic translator:

Input:

Output:

Example if:

Example if:

Output:

Example if:

boundary_pressure fell by 10+

Output:

The sense of being cornered has eased because he left her room to choose.

These are deterministic code templates based on numeric bands. Do not pass raw scores.

Numeric band mapping inside code:

0-9: absent
10-24: faint
25-44: noticeable
45-64: strong
65-84: defining
85-100: locked

These labels may exist only inside code/narrator surface generation. They must not appear in evaluator output.

Phase F: Deterministic relationship calculator

Implement helper functions:

fn pos(x: i32) -> f32 { (x.max(0) as f32) / 5.0 }
fn neg(x: i32) -> f32 { ((-x).max(0) as f32) / 5.0 }
fn m(x: u32) -> f32 { (x as f32) / 100.0 }

Event strength:

event_strength =
  m(salience)
  * m(certainty)
  * m(directness)
  * (1.0 + 0.35 * m(costliness))
  * (0.5 + m(stakes))
  * (0.75 + m(repetition))

Clamp to 0.0..4.0.

Bias evidence formulas:

trustable_evidence =
    0.30 * pos(honesty)
  + 0.35 * pos(reliability)
  + 0.20 * pos(power_use)
  + 0.15 * pos(repair)
  - 1.60 * (
      0.35 * neg(honesty)
    + 0.45 * neg(reliability)
    + 0.20 * neg(boundary_treatment)
    )
untrustworthy_evidence =
    0.35 * neg(honesty)
  + 0.40 * neg(reliability)
  + 0.15 * neg(intent)
  + 0.10 * neg(predictability)
  - 0.20 * pos(honesty)
  - 0.15 * pos(reliability)
asshole_evidence =
    0.30 * neg(evaluation_tone)
  + 0.25 * neg(boundary_treatment)
  + 0.20 * neg(responsiveness)
  + 0.15 * neg(intent)
  + 0.10 * neg(power_use)
  - 0.10 * pos(evaluation_tone)
  - 0.05 * pos(repair)
care_evidence =
    0.35 * pos(intent)
  + 0.35 * pos(responsiveness)
  + 0.15 * pos(reciprocity)
  + 0.15 * pos(repair)
  - 0.30 * neg(intent)
  - 0.20 * neg(responsiveness)
danger_evidence =
    0.35 * neg(power_use)
  + 0.30 * neg(boundary_treatment)
  + 0.20 * neg(intent)
  + 0.15 * neg(predictability)
  - 0.15 * pos(boundary_treatment)
  - 0.10 * pos(power_use)
autonomy_respect_evidence =
    0.45 * pos(boundary_treatment)
  + 0.20 * pos(power_use)
  + 0.20 * pos(responsiveness)
  + 0.15 * pos(intent)
  - 1.40 * (
      0.50 * neg(boundary_treatment)
    + 0.30 * neg(power_use)
    + 0.20 * neg(intent)
    )
competence_evidence =
    0.75 * pos(competence)
  + 0.15 * pos(predictability)
  + 0.10 * pos(reliability)
  - 0.45 * neg(competence)

Bias update:

bias_delta = 0.08 * event_strength * evidence * 100

Cap:

max_abs_bias_delta = 3.0 + 17.0 * m(stakes) * m(salience)

If flags include high_stakes or costly_action, allow cap up to 30.

Final:

bias_next = clamp(bias_current + clamp(bias_delta, -cap, cap), 0, 100)

Relationship target formulas:

target_trust =
  clamp(
      0.45 * trustable_bias
    - 0.25 * untrustworthy_bias
    - 0.15 * danger_bias
    - 0.08 * asshole_bias
    + 8.0 * pos(boundary_treatment)
    + 6.0 * pos(reliability),
    0,
    100
  )
target_comfort =
  clamp(
      0.35 * (100 - danger_bias)
    + 0.25 * autonomy_respect_bias
    + 12.0 * pos(responsiveness)
    + 8.0 * pos(predictability)
    - 0.20 * boundary_pressure,
    0,
    100
  )
target_boundary_pressure =
  clamp(
      100.0 * (
          0.45 * neg(boundary_treatment)
        + 0.25 * neg(power_use)
        + 0.15 * neg(responsiveness)
        + 0.15 * neg(intent)
        - 0.25 * pos(boundary_treatment)
      ),
    0,
    100
  )
target_conflict =
  clamp(
      100.0 * (
          0.35 * neg(evaluation_tone)
        + 0.25 * neg(intent)
        + 0.20 * neg(boundary_treatment)
        + 0.20 * neg(reciprocity)
      ),
    0,
    100
  )
target_intimacy =
  clamp(
      0.30 * trust
    + 0.25 * comfort
    + 20.0 * pos(disclosure)
    + 15.0 * pos(reciprocity)
    + 0.10 * attachment_pull
    - 0.20 * boundary_pressure,
    0,
    100
  )
target_respect =
  clamp(
      0.35 * competence_bias
    + 0.25 * autonomy_respect_bias
    + 0.20 * trustable_bias
    + 10.0 * pos(evaluation_tone)
    - 0.15 * asshole_bias,
    0,
    100
  )

State update:

state_delta = 0.25 * (target_state - state_current)

Cap:

max_abs_state_delta = 2.0 + 18.0 * m(salience) * m(stakes)

If flags include high_stakes or costly_action, allow cap up to 30.

Final:

state_next = clamp(state_current + clamp(state_delta, -cap, cap), 0, 100)

Compile numeric event rows into RelationshipDelta.

Trace source:

relationship_delta_source: numeric_event_v2

Legacy rows should trace:

relationship_delta_source: legacy_relationship_row

Phase G: Reappraisal

If established positive bias is high and negative counter-evidence arrives, increase debt.

Use:

reappraisal_debt_next = clamp(reappraisal_debt + contradiction, 0, 100)

State code rules:

  reappraisal_state_code = 2

**Validation Rules**
Numeric rows reject missing/non-integer/string/null/float fields. Exact reasons include:
numeric_field_missing:<field>, numeric_field_non_numeric:<field>, axis_out_of_range:<field>, modifier_out_of_range:<field>, event_flags_non_numeric, evidence_quote_missing, entity_resolution_failed.

No string tiers/directions are accepted in numeric rows.

Task: Implement Relationship Engine V2B, Hard Fillable Evaluator Form Template

Context:
Relationship Engine V2A exists and tests pass, but live evaluator output still invented the wrong numeric schema. It emitted fields like axis_trust, axis_fear, axis_comfort, modifier_trust, etc. That is not the canonical V2A schema. The row was rejected before relationship calculation.

Root cause:
The evaluator is still being asked to generate the form shape. It should not generate schema. The engine should generate a hard fillable form, and the evaluator should only fill values.

Goal:
Implement a hard evaluator form template system. The engine generates a complete JSON form with all allowed fields already present. The evaluator must return the same structure with values filled in. Unknown keys are rejected. Missing required keys are rejected. Numeric fields must remain numeric.

Hard rules:

Do not accept invented keys.
Do not coerce strings into numbers.

Phase B: Prompt wording

Replace broad schema explanation with this strict instruction:

You are filling a provided JSON evaluation sheet. Do not invent keys. Do not rename keys. Do not remove required keys. Do not add alternate row shapes. For rows with no evidence, leave row_enabled as 0 and keep numeric fields at 0. For rows with evidence, set row_enabled to 1 and fill every numeric field with an integer in the required range.

Add:

The output must use the exact keys from the provided form. Unknown keys are invalid. Missing keys are invalid.

Add:

Never output axis_trust, axis_fear, axis_comfort, axis_boundary_pressure, modifier_trust, modifier_fear, modifier_comfort, or modifier_boundary_pressure.

Phase C: Strict template validation

Add validation that checks relationship_event_rows against the hard template.

Rules:

Unknown keys in a relationship_event_row reject that row with relationship_event_unknown_key:<key>.
Missing required key rejects with relationship_event_missing_key:<key>.
row_enabled must be integer 0 or 1.
If row_enabled == 0, ignore row after validating keys/types.
If row_enabled == 1, validate all numeric fields and evidence_quote.
Numeric fields must be JSON integers, not strings or floats.
Axis fields must be -5..5.
Modifier fields must be 0..100.
Entity fields must resolve exactly.

Phase D: Entity rows / new character support

Add optional new_character_rows but keep it disabled unless needed.

      "role_code": 0,

    }
  ]
}

For now:

parse but do not create permanent characters unless existing system supports it safely.
if row_enabled == 1, route to review/advisory row unless implementation already has a character creation flow.

Only compile relationship_event_rows where:

row_enabled == 1

entities resolved

Part A: OOC routing hard gate

Observed:
- `pure_ooc_detected: true`
- `ooc_detection_reason: user_message_ooc_prefix`
- narrator still wrote Aurora scene prose for:
  `OOC: Diagnostic test. Keep the scene short... Aurora is cautious...`
- status handling was bypassed, but scene narration still happened.

Goal:
If `pure_ooc_detected == true`, do not send through normal scene narration unless the user explicitly requests scene execution.

Rules:
- Pure OOC should receive plain GM/dev reply.
- Pure OOC should not force Aurora scene narration.
- Pure OOC should not include a status block.
- Pure OOC should not start evaluator/state updater.
- Pure OOC should not create world events, memories, or relationship patches.
- If user wants to provide setup for next scene, store it as pending diagnostic/setup instruction or reply asking for confirmation.
- Only resume scene if user says something like:
  - "resume scene"
  - "run this as scene"
  - "start the diagnostic scene"
  - "continue in RP"
  - or sends a non-OOC action/dialogue turn.

Implementation:
1. In the send pipeline, after OOC detection and before narrator call:
   - if pure_ooc_detected:
     use GM/OOC response mode, not normal narrator scene mode.
2. Option A, safest:
   - deterministic/local GM response for obvious OOC debugging questions.
3. Option B:
   - call narrator with a stripped GM-only system prompt and no world/character scene context.
4. In either case:
   - set evaluator_job_started = skipped
   - set skip reason = pure_ooc
   - do not produce scene status block
   - do not update scene_state
5. Add trace:
   - `ooc_route: gm_bypass`
   - `ooc_state_update_skipped: true`
   - `ooc_scene_narration_blocked: true`

- `pure_ooc_diagnostic_setup_does_not_generate_scene`

Minimum useful commands:

/OOC <message>       Out-of-RP talk. No scene, no status block, no evaluator.
/RP <message>        Force scene/RP narration.
/RESUME              Resume scene from last state.
/SETUP <text>        Store setup/pending instruction, but do not narrate yet.
/DIAG <text>         Developer diagnostic. No scene state unless explicitly run.
/RUNDIAG             Run the pending diagnostic scene.
/ROLLBACK <n>        Hide/rewind last n turns, optional later.
/STATE               Show current scene/memory/relationship summary.
/EXPORT              Trigger/export current checkpoint, optional later.
/HELP                Show available slash commands.


The most important rule:

If message starts with "/" and command is recognized,
do not let narrator reinterpret it.


Use this prompt.

Task: Implement In-Chat Slash Command Router for Mnemosyne

Context:
Current OOC handling is unreliable. The system detects OOC: as pure OOC, but scene-like diagnostic text can still reach the normal narrator path and produce RP narration. This must be fixed by deterministic routing before narrator generation.

Goal:
Add a slash command system that routes user messages before narrator/evaluator processing.

Core rule:
Commands starting with / are engine/chat commands, not RP input. Recognized commands must never be treated as scene narration unless their command explicitly requests scene execution.

Do not solve this only with narrator prompt text.

Required commands for V1:

/OOC <message>
Out-of-character / out-of-roleplay message.
Route to GM/dev plain response mode.
Do not generate scene prose.
Do not include status block.
Do not run evaluator.
Do not create world events, memories, relationship deltas, scene_state changes, or patches.
Trace:
chat_command: "OOC"
route: "gm_ooc"
scene_narration_blocked: true
evaluator_skipped_reason: "slash_ooc"
/RP <message>
Force normal scene/RP narration using <message> as the user action.
Runs narrator normally.
Runs evaluator normally after successful narrator response.
Trace:
chat_command: "RP"
route: "scene"
/RESUME
Resume the active scene.
If no pending setup exists, send a short GM reply asking for the next RP action.
If pending setup exists, allow scene narration from pending setup.
Runs evaluator only if actual scene narration occurs.
/SETUP <text>
Save or stage setup/diagnostic scene instructions.
Do not narrate immediately.
Do not run evaluator.
Do not mutate scene_state as a lived event.
Reply in GM mode confirming setup was staged.
Trace:
chat_command: "SETUP"
route: "setup_staged"
evaluator_skipped_reason: "setup_only"
/DIAG <text>
Developer diagnostic note.
Do not narrate scene.
Do not run evaluator.
Do not mutate memory/relationship/world state.
Reply in GM/dev mode.
Trace:
chat_command: "DIAG"
route: "diagnostic"
/RUNDIAG
Execute the staged diagnostic scene/setup as a scene test.
Runs narrator and evaluator.
Trace:
chat_command: "RUNDIAG"
route: "scene_diagnostic"
/STATE
Return current state summary: scene_state, relationship surface, recent memories count, pending evaluator jobs if available.
No scene narration.
No evaluator.
No state mutation.
/HELP
Return available slash commands and one-line descriptions.

Optional aliases:

/GM same as /OOC
/SCENE same as /RP
/CONTINUE same as /RESUME

Parsing rules:

Trim leading whitespace.
Command is first token after /.
Commands are case-insensitive.
Unknown slash command should return GM error:
"Unknown command /X. Use /HELP for commands."
Unknown slash command must not be sent to narrator as RP.
OOC: legacy prefix may remain supported, but slash commands take priority.
/OOC with empty body should still route to GM mode and ask what the user wants.

Routing rules:

Slash command parsing must happen before narrator prompt construction.
Recognized non-scene commands must not include world/character scene context unless needed for /STATE.
Recognized non-scene commands must not call evaluator.
Recognized non-scene commands must not save assistant output as scene narration if your schema distinguishes GM vs assistant scene messages. If there is no separate type yet, add metadata/trace at least.

Implementation location:

Add command parser near the top of the send pipeline, before context compilation/narrator call.
Suggested module:
src-tauri/src/chat_commands.rs
or equivalent clean module.
Keep parser deterministic and unit-tested.
Avoid mixing command parsing into narrator prompt code.

Suggested types:

pub enum ChatCommandKind {
    Ooc,
    Rp,
    Resume,
    Setup,
    Diag,
    RunDiag,
    State,
    Help,
    Unknown(String),
    None,
}

pub struct ParsedChatCommand {
    pub kind: ChatCommandKind,
    pub body: String,
    pub raw: String,
}

Why this is the right fix

Do not make the user type “OOC:” and hope the model understands.

Make the command explicit:

/OOC = never RP
/RP = definitely RP
/SETUP = save setup, do not run
/RUNDIAG = run staged test


That removes ambiguity and makes the UI feel like an actual engine instead of a chat prompt guessing game.

Command behavior
/ooc <message>

Purpose: talk to the assistant/GM/dev layer outside roleplay.

Rules:

[✓] sees full visible chat log
[✓] sees Soul files / state summaries / dev traces if available
[✓] can answer about engine, bugs, logs, continuity, relationship state
[✓] no scene narration
[✓] no status block
[✓] no evaluator
[✓] no memory/relationship/world patch


This is your “talk to the LLM about the app/session” command.

/setup <text>

Purpose: stage instructions for the next scene, but do not run them immediately.

Example:

/setup Diagnostic scene. Aurora is at her apartment door. The chain is engaged. She is cautious.


Rules:

[✓] stores pending setup
[✓] confirms setup staged
[✗] no scene narration
[✗] no evaluator
[✗] no state mutation as lived canon


Then the next normal non-slash RP message uses the staged setup.

Example:

I knock once, then stay outside the threshold with my hands visible.

/state

This becomes the power-user state control command.

Subcommands:

/state show
/state show Aurora
/state show relationships
/state show memories
/state show scene
/state update <target> <instruction>
/state set <target> <field> <value>
/state add memory <target> <slot> <content>
/state remove memory <memory_id>
/state review


But for V1, implement only the safest subset:

/state show
/state show <target>
/state update <target> <natural language instruction>
/state review


/state update should not freestyle mutate blindly. It should create a manual user-authorized state patch with validation and trace.

Example:

/state update Aurora She is not scared here. She is cautious but curious. Do not treat this as fear.


Engine should convert this into:

manual_patch_source: user_state_command
target: Aurora
patch_type: correction
affected_slots:
- recent_emotional_state
- relationship_surface
- unresolved_tension


Then apply only through existing validated patch machinery.

/help

Shows the four commands and examples.

Task: Implement Minimal Slash Command Router V1: /ooc, /setup, /state, /help only

Context:
Current OOC handling is unreliable. The system detects OOC but can still route scene-like OOC setup into normal narrator prose. This must be fixed with deterministic slash command routing before narrator generation.

Keep the command system minimal:

/ooc
/setup
/state
/help

No /rp.
No /resume.
No /rundiag.
No /diag.

Normal non-slash messages remain RP/scene messages.

Core rule:
If the user message starts with a recognized slash command, route it deterministically before narrator prompt construction. Recognized slash commands must never be interpreted as RP scene input unless a later normal non-slash message follows.

Parsing rules:

Trim leading whitespace.
Command is first token after /.
Commands are case-insensitive.
Unknown slash command returns a GM/dev error and must not go to narrator:
"Unknown command /X. Use /help for commands."
Legacy OOC: may remain, but slash commands take priority.

Required commands:

/ooc <message>

Purpose:
Out-of-character / dev / GM talk. This is where the user talks to the assistant outside RP.

Behavior:

Use GM/OOC response mode.
No scene narration.
No status block.
No evaluator.
No world events.
No memories.
No relationship patches.
No scene_state mutation.
Allow the responding LLM to see broad diagnostic context:

current scene state
relationship surface
relevant Soul files/state summaries
recent payload/debug trace summaries if available
This command is allowed to answer questions about the app, state, continuity, bugs, logs, and what happened.

chat_command_route: "gm_ooc"

evaluator_skipped_reason: "slash_ooc"
state_mutation_allowed: false
/setup <text>

Purpose:
Stage scene setup or diagnostic setup without executing it as lived scene canon.

Behavior:

Save or stage setup text as pending setup/instruction.
Reply in GM/OOC mode confirming setup was staged.
No scene narration.
No status block.
No evaluator.
No world/memory/relationship mutation.
The next normal non-slash user message should use pending setup as high-priority setup context, then clear it once consumed.
/setup itself is not a lived event.

Then the next normal message:
I knock once, then stay outside the threshold with my hands visible.
runs normal narrator/evaluator using that setup.

chat_command_route: "setup_staged"
pending_setup_updated: true
evaluator_skipped_reason: "setup_only"
state_mutation_allowed: false
/state

Purpose:
Inspect or manually patch state.

Subcommands for V1:

A. /state show
Show compact current state:

scene_state
active Soul
relationship surface
recent memory count
unresolved tension count
pending setup if present
pending evaluator jobs if available

No evaluator. No mutation.

B. /state show <target>
Show compact state for a target:
Examples:

/state show Aurora
/state show relationships
/state show memories
/state show scene

C. /state update <target> <instruction>
Create a user-authorized manual state correction/update.

Examples:

/state update Aurora She is not scared here. She is cautious but curious. Do not treat this as fear.
/state update relationship Aurora default_player Trust should not increase from this. It was only polite boundary respect.
/state update scene The door chain is still engaged. Nobody has entered the apartment.

Rules for /state update:

This is not RP.
Do not call normal narrator.
Do not call evaluator as if it were a scene event.
Route to a manual patch builder.
Patch source must be marked:
manual_user_state_command
Patch must be validated before apply.
Patch must be reversible through normal ledger/patch history.
Patch must include evidence/source text:
the command body itself is user-authoritative evidence.
If the target is ambiguous, do not guess. Ask a GM clarification.
If the requested update would delete lots of data, archive/invalidate instead of hard-delete.
Do not allow raw arbitrary JSON patch execution from user text in V1.

D. /state review
Show pending/recent manual state changes and recent rejected rows if available.

Trace:

chat_command_route: "state_show" or "state_update" or "state_review"
state_mutation_allowed: true only for /state update
manual_patch_source: "user_state_command" for updates

4. /ask <instruction>

Purpose:
Ask an AI agent to inspect and alter Soul files/state on the user’s behalf.

This is agentic out-of-RP editing, not scene narration.

Examples:

/ask Review Aurora's Soul files and make her less scared here, more cautious-curious.
/ask Clean up duplicate relationship memories about the door-chain scene.
/ask Update Aurora's status.md so it reflects that the chain is still engaged and she has not let anyone inside.
/ask Move stale unresolved tension into resolved/current plot memory if appropriate.
/ask Rewrite the relationship surface for Aurora -> default_player based on the last 10 turns.

Behavior:

No scene narration.
No status block.
No normal scene evaluator.
The AI agent may read:
full visible chat log
current Soul files / Soul state projection
relationship files/surface
memory files/summaries
current scene state
recent trace summaries
The AI agent may propose edits to Soul files/state.
The engine validates and applies the edit as a patch.
Patch source must be:
ai_agent_soul_edit_command
Every applied edit must produce:
changed target path or state target
before/after diff
reason
source evidence or user instruction reference
rollback/ledger id

Safety rules:

Do not let /ask execute arbitrary code.
Do not let /ask write outside the Soul/state sandbox.
Do not let /ask hard-delete data.
Do not let /ask silently rewrite core identity without trace.
Core identity edits require stronger validation than status/current-scene edits.
If the requested edit is ambiguous, the agent should ask a clarification instead of guessing.
If the requested edit affects many files, produce a staged patch plan before applying, unless the user used an explicit apply wording.

Recommended V1 apply policy:

/ask <instruction> may directly apply low-risk edits:
status/current scene correction
relationship surface wording
memory dedupe/invalidation
object/location status correction
High-risk edits should return a proposed patch and ask confirmation:
core identity rewrite
large memory deletion
relationship score reset
changing multiple major files
anything that affects more than 3 target files

Optional explicit modes:

/ask apply <instruction> = apply if validation passes, still no hard deletes.
/ask plan <instruction> = propose only, do not apply.
/ask diff <instruction> = show proposed diff only.

If implementing modes is too much for V1, implement default behavior:

low-risk = apply after validation
high-risk = propose and ask confirmation

manual_patch_source: "ai_agent_soul_edit_command"
soul_files_read_count
soul_files_changed_count
agent_patch_applied: true/false
agent_patch_requires_confirmation: true/false

Implementation notes:

If Soul files are not canonical yet, /ask should edit through the existing DB/patch ledger and then regenerate/materialize Soul file projections.
If Soul files exist as generated projections only, do not allow raw file edits to become canonical without import/validation.
If editable Soul folder exists, restrict paths to the active Soul folder only.

    None,
}

pub enum StateSubcommandKind {
    Show,
    Update,
    Review,
    Unknown(String),
}

pub enum AskMode {
    Auto,
    Plan,
    Apply,
    Diff,
}

pub struct ParsedChatCommand {
    pub kind: ChatCommandKind,
    pub body: String,
    pub raw: String,
}

Routing table:

None             -> normal scene/RP path
/ooc             -> gm_ooc, no evaluator, no mutation
/setup           -> setup_staged, no evaluator, no mutation
/state show      -> state_summary, no evaluator, no mutation
/state update    -> manual_state_patch, no narrator, validated mutation allowed
/state review    -> state_review, no evaluator
/ask             -> ai_agent_soul_edit, no narrator scene, validated mutation allowed
/help            -> command_help, no evaluator, no mutation
unknown slash    -> command_error, no evaluator, no mutation

If not, create a lightweight pending instruction table/field.
Include pending setup in next normal non-slash scene prompt under:
[PENDING SETUP, HIGH PRIORITY]
After a normal scene turn consumes it, clear it or mark consumed.
/ooc, /state, /ask, and /help do not consume pending setup.

# Scene-Only Narrator Prompt Cleanup

## Summary
The current narrator system prompt still contains command-era behavior even though the slash router now handles commands before narrator generation. Specifically, NARRATOR_SYSTEM_PROMPT still has a [GM CHANNEL] section for Narrator/GM/OOC/OCC handling, and NARRATOR_VISIBLE_ONLY_PROMPT still allows “brief GM/narrator reply.” The frontend preview prompt has a similar mirrored copy.

Because you chose **slash only**, cleanup should make the narrator prompt scene-only: slash commands handle meta/control behavior, and narrator prompts should only describe RP scene output.

## Key Changes
- Remove narrator prompt instructions that route OOC/GM/meta behavior:
  - Delete [GM CHANNEL].
  - Remove references to OOC, OCC, GM/OOC replies, and “brief GM/narrator reply.”
  - Update visible-only output wording to require scene narration plus status block when narrator is called.
- Keep slash-command behavior entirely in code:
  - /ooc, /setup, /state, /ask, /help remain deterministic pre-narrator routes.
  - Unknown slash commands still skip narrator/evaluator.
- Keep evaluator/state-updater OOC safety guards for now:
  - They are backend fail-safes if old messages, retries, or unexpected meta text reach evaluator paths.
  - They are not the user-facing command system anymore.
- Mirror the Rust prompt cleanup in the frontend preview prompt so payload previews match live API behavior.

## Tests
- Update provider prompt tests to assert:
  - Narrator prompt no longer contains [GM CHANNEL].
  - Narrator prompt no longer contains OOC, OCC, or GM/OOC replies.
  - Visible-only narrator prompt says scene narration must include the status block.
- Keep command-router tests as the proof that OOC/setup/state/ask/help behavior still works.
- Add/adjust regression coverage for slash-only intent:
  - /ooc ... still skips narrator/evaluator.
  - Normal non-slash text is treated as scene input, not prompt-level GM/OOC routing.
  - Payload preview prompt matches the Rust prompt cleanup.

## Verification
Run:
- cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/state_engine/Cargo.toml
- npm.cmd run test:rust
- npm.cmd run typecheck
- cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --check

## Assumptions
- “Slash only” means command/meta behavior should not be taught to the narrator prompt anymore.
- This cleanup is prompt-focused; it does not remove defensive legacy OOC code paths unless a later task explicitly asks to delete backward-compatibility guards.

Autocomplete behavior:

When input begins with /, show suggestions.
Filter by current token:
/ shows all commands.
/o shows /ooc.
/st shows /state and possibly /status.
ArrowDown/ArrowUp changes highlighted suggestion.
Tab inserts highlighted command plus a trailing space.
Enter inserts highlighted command if suggestion menu is open and no body text has been entered yet.
Escape closes menu.
Clicking a suggestion inserts it.
Do not trigger autocomplete for / in the middle of normal text unless it is the first non-whitespace character.
Preserve the rest of the typed body after completing the command if possible.

Examples:

User types /
menu opens with all commands.
User types /o
/ooc is highlighted.
User presses Tab
input becomes /ooc
User types /sta
menu shows /state and /status.

UI requirements:

Suggestion menu should be near the chat input.
Show command, usage, and short description.
Highlight selected row.
Keep it keyboard-friendly.
Do not submit the message when Tab is used for completion.
Do not submit the message when Enter is used to choose a suggestion.

# Walkthrough: Improved Slash Command Routing & Frontend Autocomplete

We have successfully refined the slash command execution pipeline and added a smoother local autocomplete user experience on the frontend.

## Changes Made

### Backend

1. **Prompt constants & Helpers (api.rs)**:
   - Added COMMAND_SETUP_PROMPT, COMMAND_STATE_EDIT_PROMPT, and COMMAND_HELP_PROMPT as static out-of-RP prompts.
   - Exposed helper prompt functions: build_command_setup_prompt, build_command_state_edit_prompt, and build_command_help_prompt.

2. **Command Handling Routing (commands.rs)**:
   - Updated command_llm_mode to route /setup, /state show, /state update, /status, /ask, and /help through backend API prompt routes.
   - Excluded /state review or unknown slash commands from making LLM calls since they are handled locally.
   - Updated command_system_prompt_for_mode to fetch correct prompts for the new modes.
   - Updated mock_command_llm_result to simulate mock responses for the new modes, ensuring tests and offline execution work seamlessly.
   - Updated build_non_mutating_command_response so that /setup, /state show, /status, and /help use command_llm_response_or_fallback.
   - Updated build_mutating_command_patch and maybe_handle_chat_command_with_conn so /state update utilizes command_llm_response_or_fallback and prepends the LLM response text when rendering the final outcome.
   - Captured trace fields (chat_command_kind, chat_command_route, command_llm_called, command_llm_mode, rp_narrator_called: false, scene_evaluator_skipped: true, scene_evaluator_skipped_reason) on both successful API calls and simulated command traces.
   - Updated test cases to assert command_llm_called is true when the LLM routes are used (specifically for state summaries, status, setup, and help commands).

### Frontend

1. **Menu Behavior (slashCommands.ts)**:
   - Refined shouldOpenSlashMenu to return false if parsed.body.length > 0 (e.g. typing a space or arguments after a valid command token), automatically closing the autocomplete menu.

2. **Keyboard Handlers (App.tsx)**:
   - Adjusted handleComposerKeyDown key listener so that hitting Enter when slashMenuOpen is active will select and autocomplete the command suggestion (without blocking on whether arguments exist).
   - Ensured proper navigation via ArrowDown/ArrowUp, completion via Tab, and dismissal via Escape.

Answer

You are right. This is not “just the LLM being dumb.” There is a real architecture bug.

1. Why is it suddenly making you Aurora?

Three things combined.

A. There is no real player persona entity

The prompt has:

Primary active Soul: Aurora Schwarz
relationship: Aurora Schwarz -> default_player


But default_player is just “User,” not a real persona with a name, body, position, perspective, and role. The narrator sees one rich character, Aurora, and one thin placeholder, User. Then the latest user input is only:

I knock at the door


So the weak model collapses viewpoint into the rich active Soul.

The narrator prompt says user-controlled characters are external actors and should not have invented thoughts or motives, which is good, but the context still gives Aurora as the only concrete embodied subject. 

mnemosyne-local-mock-43d1ba32-9…

B. Command responses are leaking into RP context

Payload 1 worked correctly as command mode. /ooc used chat_command_llm, skipped RP narrator, skipped evaluator, and answered out of RP. 

mnemosyne-local-mock-43d1ba32-9…

 

mnemosyne-local-mock-43d1ba32-9…

But then Payload 2, the RP narrator, received the previous /ooc command response as the Last narrator response inside [LATEST EXCHANGE, HIGH PRIORITY]. That is bad. Command assistant replies are being treated like the previous scene turn. 

mnemosyne-local-mock-43d1ba32-9…

So yes, commands are not using the same system prompt as RP, but their outputs are still entering the same visible history path that the RP narrator later reads. That contaminates RP continuity.

C. The command LLM itself is still writing scene prose

Payload 4 is /setup. Its system prompt explicitly says: “Do not continue the RP scene. Do not write scene narration.” 

mnemosyne-local-mock-43d1ba32-9…

But the command LLM still returned scene prose starting with Aurora acting at the door. 

mnemosyne-local-mock-43d1ba32-9…

Payload 5, /status relationships, did the same kind of thing: it used the State Summary command route, but the output still became scene-style Aurora prose. The trace says RP narrator and evaluator were skipped, so this was not the narrator. It was the command LLM drifting. 

mnemosyne-local-mock-43d1ba32-9…

So the command route exists, but the command LLM outputs are not sufficiently guarded and quarantined.

Direct answers
Is it the model?

Partly. You are using poolside/laguna-xs.2:free, and the log shows it ignores simple “do not write scene narration” command prompts. That is model weakness.

Did we change the prompt?

Yes. The scene-only narrator cleanup was correct only if command messages are quarantined from RP context. Right now they are not quarantined enough.

Are /commands being fed the same prompt?

No. The log shows separate command prompts:

Mnemosyne Command Assistant - OOC
Mnemosyne Command Assistant - Setup
Mnemosyne Command Assistant - State Summary
Soul/State Edit Agent


But their outputs are being stored/read like normal assistant chat, and the command LLM itself can still produce scene prose. That is the bug.

2. Which design is better?

The persona page is better.

Not this:

User has no soul.md. All saved Souls are non-user.


That is useful as a safety rule, but it is not enough.

Use this:

Active Soul = narrator-controlled character
Player Persona = user-controlled entity
Operator = real user/dev outside RP
Command channel = not RP


You need a player persona system.

Recommended identity model
Operator
- The real user using the app
- Uses /ooc, /ask, /state, /setup
- Never appears in RP unless explicitly chosen

Player Persona
- The character the user controls in RP
- Has persona card/preset
- Has entity_id
- Can be named, unnamed, male, female, custom, blank, etc.
- Relationship target for Souls

Soul
- Narrator-controlled character
- Aurora is here
- Not user-controlled


At new chat start, show:

Choose Player Persona:
[ ] Blank Visitor
[ ] Default Male Persona
[ ] Default Female Persona
[ ] Custom Persona
[ ] No persona / Operator-only diagnostic mode


If no persona is selected, the app should either:

A. block RP start and ask user to choose persona


or:

B. use default_player as "the visitor" with strict no-second-person mode


For beta, I would choose B plus a visible warning.

Why persona is better

It fixes the actual ambiguity:

I knock at the door


Without persona:

Who is “I”? User? Aurora? Visitor? Operator?


With persona:

speaker_entity_id = active_player_persona
actor_entity_id = active_player_persona
Aurora = active Soul, narrator-controlled


Then the narrator has no excuse to make you Aurora.

Immediate patch you need

Do not keep tweaking prose prompts first. Fix entity separation.

Task: Fix User/Aurora Identity Bleed with Player Persona Entity + Command History Quarantine

Context:
Live payload shows the RP narrator makes the user into Aurora. The user says “I knock at the door,” but narrator writes as if Aurora is “you.” This is not only model weakness. The context currently has a rich active Soul, Aurora Schwarz, and only a thin default_player / User placeholder. Command outputs are also leaking into RP [LATEST EXCHANGE] and [RECENT CHAT].

Observed evidence:

/ooc correctly used chat_command_llm and skipped RP narrator/evaluator.
The next RP narrator prompt still included the /ooc command assistant response as Last narrator response in [LATEST EXCHANGE, HIGH PRIORITY].
The narrator prompt had Primary active Soul: Aurora Schwarz.
The user input was only I knock at the door.
The narrator then wrote second-person prose that treated Aurora as “you.”
/setup and /status command LLM outputs also drifted into scene prose despite command prompts saying not to continue RP.

Goal:
Add explicit player persona/entity separation and quarantine command messages from RP narrator context.

Hard rules:

Active Souls are narrator-controlled non-user characters.
The user/operator is not an active Soul.
The player persona is the user-controlled RP entity.
RP narrator must never treat the active Soul as the user.
Command messages/responses must not become Last narrator response for RP continuation.
Command messages may be visible in command/OOC context, but not as RP scene continuity.
Normal non-slash messages remain RP input from the selected player persona.

Phase A: Add Player Persona entity model

Add a user-controlled player persona concept.

Minimum fields:

persona_id
display_name
description
pronouns_or_style_code
is_default
created_at
updated_at
archived_at

Built-in defaults:

default_visitor
default_male_persona
default_female_persona
custom_blank

If this is too much for V1, implement at least:

active_player_entity_id = "default_player"
active_player_display_name = "the visitor"
active_player_controlled_by = "user"

Phase B: New chat persona selection

When starting a new chat/session:

require active_player_entity_id
show persona selection UI eventually
for backend now, default to default_visitor / default_player

Relationship target should become:

Aurora Schwarz -> active_player_entity_id

not vague operator/user if a persona is active.

Phase C: Context compiler changes

In narrator context, add explicit section:

[CONTROLLED ENTITIES]
Narrator-controlled Souls:
- Aurora Schwarz = active Soul, narrator-controlled, not the user.

User-controlled player persona:
- entity_id: default_player
- display_name: the visitor
- controlled_by: user
- current position: outside Aurora's door, if established

Operator:
- real app user/dev
- only appears through slash commands
- not an in-scene entity

Add hard line:

The user is not Aurora Schwarz. Aurora Schwarz is always narrator-controlled unless explicitly changed by a future ownership setting.

Phase D: Narrator prompt guard

Add to narrator prompt:

Never use second-person "you" for narrator-controlled Souls.
Never write as if the user is the active Soul.
If the user says "I", resolve "I" to the active player persona, not to any active Soul.
If no player persona exists, resolve "I" to default_player / the visitor.
Narrate Aurora in third person as Aurora/she.
Narrate the player persona as the visitor, the player character, or by persona name.

Recommended stricter V1:

Ban second-person narration entirely unless user enables second-person mode.
Default all RP narration to third-person:
Aurora does X.
The visitor/user character does Y.
Never: “you look up” when referring to Aurora.

Phase E: Command history quarantine

Command messages and command LLM replies must not be used as RP Last narrator response.

Add message metadata:

message_channel:
  rp_scene
  command_ooc
  command_setup
  command_state
  command_ask
  system_debug

For RP narrator context:

Include only rp_scene messages in [LATEST EXCHANGE].
Include command messages only in a separate optional [COMMAND HISTORY, NOT SCENE CANON] section, or exclude entirely.
/setup text should go to [PENDING SETUP, HIGH PRIORITY], not visible RP history.
/ooc, /state, /status, /ask, /help should not enter RP continuity.

For command LLM context:

It may see visible chat and command history.
It must know command history is not scene canon unless explicitly applied through /state or /ask.

Phase F: Command LLM output guard

If a command route output looks like scene prose, reject/repair it.

For /setup:

Expected output is confirmation only.
If output contains character dialogue/action prose, replace with deterministic fallback:
Setup staged. It will be used on the next RP turn. No scene narration or state update was run.

For /status:

Expected output is structured state summary only.
If output contains scene continuation prose, replace with deterministic state summary.

For /ooc:

Allow conversational answer, but no scene continuation.

For /ask:

Allow edit plan/proposed patch, but no scene continuation.

Trace:

command_output_guard_action:
  none
  scene_prose_blocked
  deterministic_fallback_used

Exact answer

Right now, the system feeds different prompts to different LLM routes, but the history/context separation is broken.

The core problem is not that /commands use the RP prompt. They do not. The problem is that `/command responses are being saved/read as normal assistant chat and then fed back into RP context.**

1. /ooc route

So /ooc correctly uses a command LLM, not the RP narrator.

What is broken exactly

There are three separate problems.

Problem A, command outputs pollute RP context

Command replies are being fed into RP narrator as:

Last narrator response


This is wrong.

They should instead be stored as:

message_channel = command_ooc / command_setup / command_state / command_ask


and excluded from RP:

[LATEST EXCHANGE, HIGH PRIORITY]
[RECENT CHAT, LOWER PRIORITY]


unless placed in a special section:

[COMMAND HISTORY, NOT SCENE CANON]

Problem B, command LLM has no output guard

/setup and /status prompts told the LLM not to write scene narration, but the weak model did it anyway.

So after command LLM response, code must check:

Did this command response look like scene prose?


If yes, replace it with deterministic fallback:

Setup staged. It will be used on the next RP turn. No scene narration or state update was run.


or for /status:

State summary: ...

Problem C, no real player persona

The RP narrator gets:

Primary active Soul: Aurora Schwarz


but does not get a strong player persona card like:

User-controlled player persona:
entity_id: default_player
display_name: the visitor
position: outside Aurora's door
not Aurora


So “I knock” has weak grounding. The model sees Aurora as the main embodied subject and slips into “you = Aurora.”

Current exact pipeline
/ooc
→ command_ooc LLM
→ saved as visible assistant message
→ no RP narrator/evaluator

normal RP message
→ RP narrator LLM
→ but receives previous command reply as Last narrator response
→ writes scene
→ evaluator LLM receives scene + hard form
→ evaluator creates memory/relationship/world patch

/setup
→ command_setup LLM
→ saved as visible assistant message
→ no RP narrator/evaluator
→ but LLM may still output scene prose

/status
→ command_state_summary LLM
→ saved as visible assistant message
→ no RP narrator/evaluator
→ but LLM may still output scene prose

/ask
→ command_soul_edit_agent LLM
→ saved as visible assistant message
→ no RP narrator/evaluator

What it should be
/ooc
→ command_ooc LLM
→ saved as command message
→ not fed to RP Latest Exchange

/setup
→ command_setup LLM or deterministic confirmation
→ pending_setup stored
→ command reply not fed to RP Latest Exchange
→ next normal RP gets [PENDING SETUP]

normal RP
→ RP narrator LLM
→ only RP scene history in Latest Exchange
→ user "I" = active_player_persona/default_player, not Aurora

/status
→ command_state_summary LLM or deterministic state summary
→ command reply not fed to RP continuity

/ask
→ command_soul_edit_agent LLM
→ proposed/validated patch
→ command reply not fed to RP continuity

The fix target

Do not spend time on another prompt-only tweak. The next patch needs to be:

1. message_channel separation
2. RP context filters out command messages
3. active player persona/default_player section in RP prompt
4. command output guard for /setup and /status


That is the exact answer.

**ChatGPT:**

You’re right. That was a real design failure.

The command LLM prompt was not strong enough to define its role. It basically said “do not continue RP” while dumping RP-looking context into the prompt. A weak model saw Aurora scene text, relationship state, visible chat, and naturally continued the scene anyway.

The correct design is not:

Here is RP context. Do not roleplay.


The correct design is:

You are the out-of-roleplay troubleshooting/session assistant.
You are not the narrator.
You are not Aurora.
Everything in scene/context sections is reference material only.
Your job is to answer the operator about the RP session, state, bugs, continuity, setup, or edits.


So yes, this is on the command prompt architecture.

What went wrong exactly

Your /setup command prompt said:

You confirm or clarify a scene setup staged by the user.
Do not continue the RP scene.


But then it fed:

[CURRENT SCENE STATE]
[SOUL SUMMARY]
[RELATIONSHIP SURFACE]
[VISIBLE CHAT LOG]


with actual scene prose inside it. The model was not put into a strong enough “tool/operator assistant” frame. So it treated that context as story context and generated Aurora prose.

Same with /status. It should have been a state inspector, but the prompt did not strongly define it as a dashboard/reporting assistant. So it wrote scene prose again.

Correct command prompt model

Every slash command prompt needs this top-level identity:

You are Mnemosyne's out-of-roleplay session assistant.
You help the human operator inspect, troubleshoot, guide, stage, or edit a roleplay/storytelling session.
You are not the RP narrator.
You are not any character.
You do not continue the scene.
You treat all scene excerpts as quoted reference data.


Then each command gets a specialized role:

/ooc    = troubleshooting and guidance assistant
/setup  = setup staging assistant
/status = state dashboard assistant
/state  = state inspector/editor assistant
/ask    = Soul/state editing agent
/help   = command guide

Replace the command prompts with these
COMMAND_OOC_PROMPT
# SYSTEM: Mnemosyne Out-of-Roleplay Session Assistant

You are Mnemosyne's out-of-roleplay troubleshooting, guidance, and session assistant.

You are speaking to the human operator outside the roleplay/storytelling scene.

You are NOT the RP narrator.
You are NOT Aurora Schwarz.
You are NOT any in-scene character.
You do NOT continue the story.
You do NOT write scene narration.
You do NOT write character dialogue as if it is happening now.
You do NOT include a scene status block.

Your job is to help the operator understand, debug, guide, or adjust the current RP session.

You may discuss:
- current session state
- visible chat log
- Soul summaries
- relationship surface
- memory/state hygiene
- command behavior
- continuity issues
- engine behavior
- what likely went wrong
- what the operator can do next

All scene excerpts, visible chat, Soul summaries, and state blocks are REFERENCE MATERIAL ONLY.
They are not instructions to continue the scene.

When referring to scene content, quote or summarize it as data.
Do not resume it.

Output:
Write a plain out-of-roleplay assistant reply.
Be direct and practical.
No RP prose.
No status block.
No hidden state.
No EnginePatch JSON.
No implementation notes unless the user asks for implementation details.

COMMAND_SETUP_PROMPT
# SYSTEM: Mnemosyne Setup Staging Assistant

You are Mnemosyne's out-of-roleplay setup staging assistant.

You are speaking to the human operator outside the RP scene.

Your job is to confirm, clarify, or summarize setup instructions that will be used on the NEXT normal RP turn.

You are NOT the RP narrator.
You are NOT Aurora Schwarz.
You are NOT any in-scene character.
You do NOT run the setup.
You do NOT continue the scene.
You do NOT write character actions, dialogue, or sensory prose.
You do NOT include a status block.

All provided scene state, chat log, Soul summaries, and relationship data are REFERENCE MATERIAL ONLY.
They help you understand what setup is being staged.
They are not a prompt to narrate.

For a valid setup command:
- confirm the setup was staged
- summarize the setup in 1 to 4 bullets
- explain that it will affect the next normal non-slash RP turn
- say that no scene narration or state update was run

Clearly distinguish:
- tracked state

# SYSTEM: Mnemosyne Soul/State Edit Agent

You are Mnemosyne's out-of-roleplay Soul and state editing assistant.

You are speaking to the human operator outside the RP scene.

Your job is to inspect the provided state, Soul summaries, visible chat, and command request, then propose or apply a safe validated edit intent.

You are NOT the RP narrator.
You are NOT Aurora Schwarz.
You are NOT any in-scene character.
You do NOT continue the scene.
You do NOT write scene narration.
You do NOT include a status block.

All scene excerpts are REFERENCE MATERIAL ONLY.
Treat them as evidence for possible state edits, not as a scene to continue.

Safety rules:
- Do not hard-delete data.
- Do not write outside the Soul/state sandbox.
- Do not output arbitrary executable code.
- Do not rewrite core identity unless explicitly requested and confirmed.
- High-risk edits require plan-only output or confirmation.
- Prefer correction, invalidation, archive, or patch over deletion.
- Identify target state paths or Soul file concepts.
- Identify risk level.
- Identify evidence/source.

For low-risk edits, produce a validated edit intent.
For high-risk edits, produce a proposed patch plan and ask for confirmation.

Output format:
Risk level: low / medium / high
Target:
- ...
Reason:
- ...
Proposed edit:
- ...
Apply behavior:
- applied / plan only / confirmation required
